STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission 
From:    Brandon Brady, PE, Transportation Planning Deputy Director  
Date of Meeting:  March 12, 2024 
Type of Item:  Summit County Transportation Impact Study Guidelines 
   
 
Background: 
For larger development projects, Summit County (County) has required a Transportation Impact Study 
(TIS) to evaluate the impacts that the development will have on the surrounding roadway system.  
However, there has never been a set of rules or guidelines to follow for when a TIS is required and what 
it should include.  The Summit County Transportation Division reviewed several different TIS guidelines 
from multiple sources to create our own set of guidelines.  These guidelines were presented to the 
Eastern Summit County Planning Commission on March 7th for their review and comments. 
 
Guidelines: 
This document is meant to assist applicants when assessing transportation impacts from development 
projects.  The guidelines will provide a technical approach that will benefit both the consulting 
community and County.  This document establishes protocol f or TIS and mitigation based on 
transportation planning and engineering practices.  This is not an exhaustive list and requirements can 
change based on Summit County Planning Commissions and County Council comments throughout the 
planning process. 
 
Staff recommends that a TIS be required for any development that will generate 25 or more net new 
vehicle trips during the weekday AM or PM peak hour.  Table 1 shows the approximate land use size 
that would generate 25 new vehicle trips during the weekday PM peak hour. 
 
Table 1: Approximate Size of Development to Generate 25 Weekday PM Peak Hour Trips 
Land Use ITE Land Use Code Land Use Size 
Single Family Detached Housing 210 25 dwelling units 
Single Family Attached Housing 215 45 dwelling units 
Multifamily Housing (Mid-Rise) 221 60 dwelling units 
Hotel 310 40 rooms 
General Retail  822 3,000 sf 
Office  710 15,000 sf 
Sit-Down Restaurant 932 2,500 sf 
Fast Food / Fast Casual Restaurant 934 800 sf 
Source: ITE Trip Generation, 11th Edition

P.O. Box 128 C Coalville, UT 84017 
Coalville: (435) 336-3250 C Kamas: (435) 783-4351 ext. 3250 C Park City (435) 615-3250 
Fax: (435) 336-3043 C Park City Fax (435) 615-3043 
In the guidelines it addresses the following: 
• Project Scoping 
• Study Time Period 
• Project Trip Generation 
• Project Trip Distribution and Assignment 
• Traffic Volumes 
• Traffic Operations Analysis 
• Site Access Analysis 
• Traffic Safety 
• Non-Motorized Transportation 
• Transit Access Analysis 
 
The new areas that the guidelines address that have not been included in the past are safety, active 
transportation, and transit.  Staff believe that these are important components of transportation and 
need to be included. 
 
Discussion: 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission comments on any aspect of the TIS 
Guidelines on what they would like to clarify or change.  Staff would appreciate any comments on the 
traffic volume horizon years.  How far out would you like to see projected traffic volumes?   
 
Next Steps: 
Staff will make changes based on comments by both planning commissions and then work with the 
Planning Department staff and come back to officially adopt the guidelines into the code. 
 
Appendices: 
 
Appendix A: Summit County, Transportation Impact Study Guidelines

Appendix A 
Summit County, Transportation Impact Study Guidelines 
6
 
Transportation Impact Study Guidelines 
Introduction 
This document is meant to assist applicants when assessing transportation impacts from 
development projects.  The guidelines will provide a technical approach that will benefit both 
the consulting community and Summit County (County).  This document establishes protocol for 
transportation impact study (TIS) and mitigation based on transportation planning and 
engineering practices.  This is not an exhaustive list and requirements can change based on 
Summit County Planning Commissions and County Council comments throughout the planning 
process. 
Review Process 
TIS should be submitted to the County with the submittal of the development application as 
action will not be taken on the application until final approval of the TIS. Along with the TIS, the 
intake checklist should be completed and included with the submittal. 
The TIS shall be prepared by a professional engineer registered in the State of Utah. All work 
shall be stamped and signed. The applicant is responsible for hiring the engineer to perform the 
TIS. 
If a TIS submittal is deemed incomplete or outdated, the County will not initiate a review until 
all components are submitted. The engineer and applicant will be notified of the deficiencies in 
the submittal. This notification shall in no way constitute a review of the TIS. Once the TIS 
submittal is deemed complete, the County will provide, in writing to the engineer, applicant, 
and lead land use agency, an estimated timeline for the review. The review time needed will 
vary depending on the number of traffic studies in the “queue” and the complexity and 
accuracy of the submitted study. 
 
7
Study Requirement Threshold 
A TIS will be required prior to the approval of a project at the time of the application if the 
project will generate 25 or more net new vehicle trips during the weekday AM or PM peak hour 
or other analysis hour at the discretion of the Transportation Planning Director.  The 
Transportation Planning Director also reserves the right to require or not require a TIS for any 
other reason.   
Table 1 shows the approximate land use size that would generate 25 new vehicle trips during 
the weekday PM peak hour.  In addition to the weekday PM peak hour, the weekday AM peak 
hour should also be reviewed to estimate the trip generation of the proposed development.  
Proposed developments may take credit for vehicle trips generated by existing land uses to be 
replaced on the project site or pass by trips. 
A full listing of trip generation rates can be found in the latest version of the Institute of 
Transportation Engineers Trip Generation Manual. 
Table 1: Approximate Size of Development to Generate 25 Weekday PM Peak Hour Trips 
Land Use ITE Land Use Code Land Use Size 
Single Family Detached Housing 210 25 dwelling units 
Single Family Attached Housing 215 45 dwelling units 
Multifamily Housing (Mid-Rise) 221 60 dwelling units 
Hotel 310 40 rooms 
General Retail  822 3,000 sf 
Office  710 15,000 sf 
Sit-Down Restaurant 932 2,500 sf 
Fast Food / Fast Casual Restaurant 934 800 sf 
Source: ITE Trip Generation, 11th Edition   
 
Project Scoping 
 
Scoping is the first step when it is determined that a TIS is required for a development.  The 
consultant should meet with the Summit County Transportation Planning Division to discuss the 
items that are best suited for the type and level of analysis that will be necessary for the 
project.   
• Description of the proposed development’s land uses 
• Anticipated construction schedule 
• Preliminary site plan 
• Project location and site access 
• Estimated vehicular trip generation and mode share assumptions 
• Project trip distribution 
• Proposed study intersections 
8
• Proposed traffic volume growth rates 
• Any other relevant project-specific information 
 
Study Time Period 
 
In the Snyderville Basin area where there is seasonal traffic, the TIS should reflect a typical 
weekday during the typical winter (January to mid-March) period.  If traffic counts are taken 
outside of the typical winter weekday, then a seasonal adjustment factor may be used.  The 
seasonal adjustment factor will need to be approved by the Transportation Planning Division.  
At some intersections, it may be appropriate to increase or decrease specific movements (e.g., 
those which would or would not be affected by seasonal traffic) by higher percentages than 
others.  However, the total entering intersection volume should be adjusted consistent with the 
seasonal adjustment factor.  The methodology used to factor intersection traffic volumes should 
be described in detail in the TIS. 
 
In the rest of the county the TIS should reflect a typical weekday.  The required study time 
periods include the highest one hour of adjacent street traffic during the AM (8-10 a.m.) peak 
and PM (3:30-5:30) peak hours. 
 
Project Trip Generation 
 
Site-generated trips for all modes of travel (e.g., vehicular, walking, bicycling, and transit) 
expected for the proposed development should be estimated using the trip rates and 
methodologies described in the most recent editions of the ITE Trip Generation and ITE Trip 
Generation Handbook. 
 
When available, additional information collected from surveys of similar developments, local 
mode share data, or US Census data can also be utilized to aid in calculations of trip generation 
and mode split estimates. Assumptions used to determine the trip generation (including transit 
and non-motorized transportation mode splits), as well as and supplemental backup data, 
should be clearly described and documented in the project scoping memo for approval and in 
the completed TIS. 
 
Project Trip Distribution and Assignment 
 
The project trip distribution should be determined using the best available information for the 
area with project trips assigned to the roadway network using realistic estimates.  Where 
applicable, the latest version of the Summit/Wasatch travel demand model should be used for 
trip distribution. The methodology and assumptions used to develop the project trip 
distribution should be outlined and documented in the scoping memo and the completed TIS. 
 
 
 
 
9
Traffic Volumes 
 
The TIS horizon years should correlate with the buildout of the project, development phasing, 
and/or Summit County Long Range Transportation Plan (LRTP) phases.  Traffic Volumes should 
be developed for the following analysis scenarios: 
 
• Existing 
• Year of project opening (if different from existing) without project generated traffic 
volumes 
• Year of project opening with project generated traffic volumes 
• Five years beyond year of project opening without project generated traffic 
• Five years beyond year of project opening with project generated traffic 
• Larger projects may be required to match the different phases of the Summit County 
LRTP 
 
If a project is anticipated to be completed in phases, each anticipated phase should be 
evaluated before full buildout.  Future traffic volumes should be developed using annual growth 
rates determined by the Summit-Wasatch travel demand model for a baseline. 
 
Traffic Operations Analysis 
 
Traffic operations at study intersections should be analyzed using the most recent Highway 
Capacity Manual methodology.  Peak hour factors should be calculated and applied to the 
intersection as a whole, whereas heavy vehicle percentages should be applied by approach or 
individual movement. Each study intersection’s level of service (LOS) and average vehicle delay 
should be reported. The County requires an operational standard to be maintained at a LOS C 
for all County roads and intersections and a LOS D for State roads. 
 
In addition to vehicular LOS, the 50th and 95th percentile queue lengths and available queue 
storage should be reported for each intersection.  At intersections where the 50th percentile 
peak hour queue exceeds the available storage, the traffic operations analysis should be 
reviewed to determine if spillback queueing affects the traffic operations at other intersections 
within the roadway network. 
 
Mitigation measures should be examined for intersections the exceed the LOS standard or 
produce queues that affect traffic operations at other intersections. Software files for all analysis 
and mitigation scenarios should be submitted to the Transportation Planning Division along with 
the TIS. 
 
Site Access Analysis 
 
The site access analysis should include the following: 
 
10
• The connection of how each vehicular site access connects to the existing roadway 
system and the LOS at each access point should be evaluated.  
• The access points for people walking and biking to the proposed development should be 
described.   
• The location of commercial vehicle access for loading/unloading and deliveries should 
be evaluated. 
 
Traffic Safety 
 
Historical crash data should be summarized at each intersection for the previous five-year 
period using records from the Numetric database. Crash patterns should be identified and 
described along with anticipated changes that could occur with the proposed project’s 
development. For any proposed roadway improvements, a safety analysis using crash 
modification factor countermeasures as outlined in the AASHTO Highway Safety Manual should 
be conducted to compare the predictive safety of different types of treatments that could be 
implemented at the location. 
 
On roadways with higher speeds, turn lanes and deceleration/acceleration lanes may be 
required for safety even if the LOS thresholds are met. 
 
Non-Motorized Transportation 
 
An analysis of the pedestrian and bicycle system should be documented, shown on a site plan, 
and include all the following that apply: 
 
Pedestrian System Analysis: 
• Completeness of the pedestrian network on site 
• Connectivity of the site pedestrian network to the surrounding existing network 
• Accessibility to nearby shared off-street paths 
• Direct pedestrian routes rather than circuitous routes between pedestrian destinations 
• Gaps in the area’s existing pedestrian facilities that could prevent or discourage 
pedestrian accessing the development 
• Adequate walkway width and queuing/waiting areas 
• Separation, buffers, and barriers from motor vehicles 
• Frequency and design of street crossing opportunities 
 
Bike System Analysis: 
• Completeness of the bicycle network onsite 
• Connectivity of the site to the bicycle network of the surrounding existing network 
• Accessibility to nearby shared off-street paths 
• Direct bicycle routes rather than circuitous routes  
• Gaps in the area’s existing bicycle facilities that could prevent or discourage accessing 
the development by bicycle 
11
• Adequate bicycle way width and enough secure bicycle parking areas 
• Separation, buffers, and barriers from motor vehicles and, where appropriate, heavy 
pedestrian traffic 
 
In areas where there is high pedestrian activity, the Transportation Planning Director may 
require bicycle and pedestrian level of stress evaluation. 
 
Transit Access Analysis 
 
Existing transit stops within one-quarter mile of the proposed project should be documented. 
The estimated mode share and number of trips for people walking to transit, completed as part 
of the trip generation, should be described along with evaluating the safe walking route and 
estimated distance to access the nearby transit stops for the proposed development. 
 
Format 
 
The TIS shall be prepared by a firm or individual approved by the County as capable of 
performing a traffic analysis. 
 
The TIS should follow the recommended format below: 
 
1. Introduction and Executive Summary 
2. Proposed Project 
3. Study Area Conditions 
4. Analysis of Existing Conditions 
5. Projected Traffic 
a. Trip generation 
b. Pass-by trips 
c. Internal trips 
d. Trip distribution and assignment 
e. Non-site traffic projections 
f. Total traffic 
6. Traffic Analysis 
a. Site ingress/egress 
b. Level of Service Analysis 
7. Traffic Safety Analysis 
a. Conflict points (vehicle/vehicle, vehicle/cyclist, vehicle/pedestrian) 
b. Drive-through lanes 
8. Non-Motorized Transportation Analysis 
9. Transit Access Analysis 
10. Conclusions 
11. Recommendations 
12. Appendices 
a. Traffic Counts 
12
b. Traffic Capacity Analysis 
c. Accident Summary 
d. Request for Change of Access (if appliable) 
 
Figures and Tables to be integrated into the report: 
• Vicinity map 
• Scaled drawings showing: 
1. Geometric and physical concerns related to the project area and access points. 
a. Existing roadways and traffic control features 
b. Existing daily volumes 
c. Collision diagram summary 
d. Site generated trip summary 
e. Directional distribution of Site generated traffic 
f. Assignment of non-Site related traffic 
g. Assignment of Site traffic 
h. Traffic capacity analysis 
(1) Projected levels of service without the project to coincide with 
development years. 
(2) Projected levels of service with the project. 
i. Recommended mitigation or improvement

STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Jennifer Strader, Senior Planner 
Date of Meeting: March 12, 2024 
Type of Item: Code Amendment – Public Hearing / Possible Action 
Process:  Legislative 
 
 
RECOMMENDATION:  Staff recommends the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission 
(SBPC) review the proposed language amending specific sections of the Snyderville 
Basin Development Code (Code), conduct a public hearing, and forward a positive 
recommendation to the County Council.  
 
BACKGROUND 
As part of the Community Development Department’s workplan for 2024, Staff is 
proposing amendments to the Code to provide consistency with State Code and 
Eastern Summit County Development Code processes, fix outdated references, and 
make other necessary minor amendments. Staff determined these amendments are 
primarily administrative in nature, intended to clarify and clean up certain items. These 
amendments do not affect existing land uses, density, or other substantive development 
standards. 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS 
The proposed language is attached as Exhibit A. Reasons for the proposed changes 
are identified under each section.  
 
ANALYSIS 
Section 10-7-3 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code states that whenever there 
is initiated an amendment to the Code, it must be reviewed by the Planning Commission 
who will deliver a recommendation to the County Council. The County Council, after 
holding a public hearing, shall approve, approve with modifications, or deny the 
amendment according to the following criteria. 
 
Criteria 1: The amendment shall be consistent with the goals, objectives, and policies 
of the general plan. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed amendments provide consistency with State Code and 
Eastern Summit County Development Code processes, fix outdated references, 
17
 
 
 
 
   
and make other necessary minor adjustments. The amendments do not affect 
the goals, objectives, and policies of the general plan.  
 
Criteria 2: The amendment shall not permit the use of land that is not consistent with 
the uses of properties nearby. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed amendments do not affect existing land uses. 
 
Criteria 3: The amendment will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the 
proposed amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis:  The proposed amendments do not pertain to any uses and therefore 
will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the proposed amendment to 
the uses to which they have been restricted.  
 
Criteria 4: The amendment will not permit the removal of the then existing restrictions 
which will unduly affect nearby property. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed amendments provide consistency with State Code and 
Eastern Summit County Development Code processes, fix outdated references, 
and make other necessary minor adjustments. The amendments do not remove 
any existing restrictions. 
 
Criteria 5:  The amendment will not grant special favors or circumstances solely for one 
property owner or developer. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed amendments apply to the entire Snyderville Basin 
Planning Area. 
 
Criteria 6: The amendment will promote the public health, safety, and welfare better 
than the existing regulations for which the amendment is intended to change. 
COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed amendments provide consistency with State Code and 
Eastern Summit County Development Code processes, fix outdated references, 
and make other necessary minor adjustments. 
 
Recommendation 
Staff recommends that the Planning Commission review the attached language 
amending regulations for accessory buildings and structures in the Snyderville Basin, 
conduct a public hearing and forward a positive recommendation to the County Council.  
 
 
 
 
 
18
 
 
 
 
   
Findings of Fact 
 
1. As part of the Community Development Department’s workplan for 2024, Staff is 
proposing amendments to the Code to provide consistency with State Code and 
Eastern Summit County Development Code processes, fix outdated references, 
and make other necessary minor amendments. 
2. Staff determined these amendments are primarily administrative in nature, 
intended to clarify and clean up certain items.  
3. These amendments do not affect existing land uses, density, or other substantive 
development standards. 
4. The proposed amendments do not affect existing land uses. 
5. The amendments do not remove any existing restrictions. 
6. The proposed amendments apply to the entire Snyderville Basin Planning Area. 
 
Conclusions of Law: 
 
1. The amendment is consistent with the goals, objectives, and policies of the 
General Plan. 
2. The amendments will not permit the use of land that is not consistent with the 
uses of properties nearby. 
3. The amendments will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the 
proposed amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. 
4. The amendments will not permit the removal of the then existing restrictions 
which will unduly affect nearby property. 
5. The amendments will not grant special favors or circumstances solely for one 
property owner or developer. 
6. The amendments will promote the public health, safety, and welfare better than 
the existing regulations for which the amendments are intended to change.

Vicinity Map 
 
Background 
 
The Canyons Development Agreement allocates the use, height, and maximum allowed square 
footage for each parcel within the Resort.  The maximum allowable density according to the 
Canyons SPA and Land Use chart is 80,000 square feet of multi-family residential, a maximum of 
2.5 stories in height EXHIBIT B. 
 
• April 27, 2017, the County Manger approved the White Pine Canyon Village Final Site 
Plan, which included 30 multi-family units and a clubhouse.  
• White Pine Canyon Village Building 1 -3 Phase One condominium 
plat was recorded on July 6, 2018, and consists of (2) tri-plex and 
a duplex, units 1-8. 
• White Pine Canyon Village Building 4,  Supplemental 
Condominium Plat Phase 2  was recorded on October 12, 2021 , 
consisting of a four- plex, units 9-12. 
• White Pine Canyon Village Building 5-6, Supplemental 
Condominium Plat Phase 3  was recorded on July 6, 2022 , 
consisting of (2) duplex buildings, units 13-16.

Condominium Plat Phase 4 , was recorded on July 8, 2022 ,

White Pine Canyon Village Phase 5 Condo Plat - Discussion/Possible Recommendation – March 12, 2024 
 
• On September 1, 2023, an application was received for the proposed White Pine Canyon 
Village Building 9, Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 5, which will be a four -plex, 
containing 3 townhomes and a penthouse, units 21-24. 
 
The Canyons CVMA submitted a positive letter of recommendation for the White Pine Canyon 
Village Phase 5 on August 25, 2023 EXHIBIT B. 
 
Analysis and Findings 
  
The approval process for all subdivision plats and final site plans within the Canyons SPA area, 
including the subject parcel, are governed by the Canyons SPA Development Agreement.  
Section 2.7 of the Amended and Restated Development Agreement, adopted on November 
15th, 1999, provides the Development Approval Process for all Final Subdivision Plat or 
Condominium Plats and Final Site Plans within the SPA.  After reviewing the proposed  
Condominium Plat, the Planning Commission shall make a recommendation to the Board of 
County Commission.  This administrative function is now handled by the County Manager.  The 
County Manger will then conduct a public hearing and make a final decision of approval, 
approval with conditions or denial for White Pine Canyon Village Building 9  Supplemental 
Condominium Plat Phase 5. 
 
Staff sent the condominium plat out to service providers for review and comment.  There were 
some technical corrections that needed to be addressed on the plat and in the condominium 
declarations.  Staff sugges ts a conditioned that all service provider comments  and conditions  
must be met, prior to final signatures and recordation  of the condominium plat and 
declarations. 
 
Staff’s review of the application, based upon the information presented and with the 
recommendation of the Canyons DRC, indicates that the project complies with the Canyons 
Development Agreement and meets the minimum requirement for approval in the Snyderville 
Basin Development Code. 
 
Recommendation 
 
It is staff’s finding that the project meets the applicable standards in the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code and the Canyons Development Agreement . Staff recommends that the 
Planning Commission review the proposal for compliance with the Code. Based upon the 
review outlined in this report, staff recommends that the SBPC  make a positive 
recommendation for the White Pine Canyon Village Building 9  Supplemental Condominium Plat 
Phase 5 Condominium Plat according to the following findings of fact, conclusions of law and 
conditions of approval:

4 
White Pine Canyon Village Phase 5 Condo Plat - Discussion/Possible Recommendation – March 12, 2024 
 
Findings of Fact: 
 
1. According to the current assessor’s parcel information  White Pine Development Corp is 
the owner of record for parcel LVDAM-LV10.   
2. Parcel LVDAM-LV10 is within the Lower Village development area of the Canyons SPA. 
3. The White Pine Canyon Village Townhomes Final Site Plan was recorded on May 29, 
2018. 
4. Parcel LVDAM-LV10 is zoned Specially Planned Area (SPA) and is with the Canyons SPA 
boundaries. 
5. Parcel LVDAM-LV10 has a maximum density of 80,000 square feet that can be utilized 
for multi-family residential. 
6. The parcel is allowed to be a maximum of 2.5  stories in height, as described in the 
Canyons Development Agreement. 
7. White Pine Canyon Village Building 1 -3 Phase One condominium plat was recorded on 
July 6, 2018, and consists of (2) tri-plex and a duplex building.  
8. White Pine Canyon Village Building 4, Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 2 was 
recorded on October 12, 2021, consisting of a four- plex, containing 3 townhomes and a 
penthouse unit. 
9. White Pine Canyon Village Building 5 -6, Second Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 
3 was recorded on July 6, 2022, consisting of (2) Duplex buildings. 
10. White Pine Canyon Village Building 7 -8, Second Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 
4 accounting for 20 multi -family units, was recorded on July 8, 2022 , consisting of (2) 
duplex buildings.  
11. On ___, an application was received for the proposed White Pine Canyon Village 
Building 9, Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 5 will be a four- plex, containing 3 
townhomes and a penthouse unit. 
12. The Canyons CVMA submitted a positive letter of recommendation for the White Pine 
Canyon Village Phase 5 on August 25, 2023 
13. Section 57 -8-13 of the Utah State Code requires recordation of a condominium plat 
prior to the sale of individual residential units. 
 
Conclusions of Law: 
 
1. The condominium plat is consistent with all prior approvals for the project and 
necessary for the units to be sold separately.  
2. The White Pine Canyon Village Building 9 ,  Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 5  
complies with all requirements of the Snyderville Basin Development Code and the 
Canyons DA.

RE: LV10 – White Pine Canyon Townhomes Phase Six 
 
Mrs. Northrup-Robinson, 
 
The CVMA has received a request to review  Phase Six for the White Pine Canyon Townhome 
development on parcel LV10. The phasing plan is in accordance with the DRC approved final site 
plan and CVMA recommends  a positive recommendation to move forward with Phase Six  as 
presented on the attached proposed plat and subject to the following conditions:  
 
1) Compliance with section 2.9.4 of the SPA Development Agreement outlining concurrency 
with Summit County Code as well as the SPA Development and Management Agreements. 
 
2) The developer will contact the CVMA in writing for a property inspection to be 
scheduled 30 days prior to the issuance of any Certificate of Occupancy for any of the 
phases. As part of this inspection process, the applicant must fill and sign a CVMA 
Density Verification Form for all units scheduled for inspection. 
 
Thank you, and please contact me with any questions, 
 
 
On behalf of the Design Review Committee: 
 
 
 
 
John Simmons

Analysis and Findings 
The approval process for all subdivision plats and final site plans within the Canyons SPA area, 
including the subject parcel, are governed by the Canyons SPA Development Agreement. 
Section 2.7 of the Amended and Restated Development Agreement, adopted on November 
15
th, 1999, provides the Development Approval Process for all Final Subdivision Plat or 
Condominium Plats and Final Site Plans within the SPA.  After reviewing the proposed  
Condominium Plat, the Planning Commission shall make a recommendation to the Board of 
County Commission.  This administrative function is now handled by the County Manager.  The 
County Manger will then conduct a public hearing and make a final decision of approval, 
approval with conditions or denial for White Pine Canyon Village Building 11  Supplemental 
Condominium Plat Phase 7. 
S
taff sent the condominium plat out to service providers for review and comment.  There were 
some technical corrections that needed to be addressed on the plat and in the condominium 
declarations.  Staff sugges ts a conditioned that all service provider comments  and conditions  
must be met, prior to final signatures and recordation  of the condominium plat and 
declarations. 
S
taff’s review of the application, based upon the information presented and with the 
recommendation of the Canyons DRC, indicates that the project complies with the Canyons 
Development Agreement and meets the minimum requirement for approval in the Snyderville 
Basin Development Code. 
Recommendation

4

review outlined in this report, staff recommends that the SBPC  make a positive 
recommendation for the White Pine Canyon Village Building 11  Supplemental Condominium

Fi
ndings of Fact: 
1. Ac
cording to the current assessor’s parcel information  White Pine Development Corp is

development area of the Canyons SPA.
3. The White Pine Canyon Village Townhomes Final Site Plan was recorded on May 29,
2018.
4. Parcel WPCV-EXP-LAND is zoned Specially Planned Area (SPA) and is with the Canyons
SPA boundaries.
5. LV10 has a maximum density of 80,000 square feet that can be utilized for multi- family
residential, according to the Canyons Development Agreement Land Use and Zoning
Chart.
6. The parcel is allowed to be a maximum of 2.5  stories in height, as described in the
Canyons Development Agreement Land Use and Zoning Chart.
7. On April 27, 2017, the County Manger approved the White Pine Canyon Village Final Site
Plan, which included 30 multi-family units and a clubhouse.
8. White Pine Canyon Village Building 1 -3 Phase 1 condominium plat was recorded on July
6, 2018, and consists of (2) tri-plex and a duplex, units 1-8.
9. White Pine Canyon Village Building 4, Supplemental Condominium Plat Phase 2 was
recorded on October 12, 2021, consisting of a four- plex, units 9-12.

STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Tiffanie N. Robinson, Senior Planner 
Date of Meeting: March 12, 2024 
Type of Item:  Canyons Village Parking Garage - Low Impact Permit 
Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation  
Process:  Administrative Review 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:  
Summit County Planning Department has received a Low Impact Permit request for  the 
construction of the Canyons Village Parking Garage  to be located at 1840 Ozzy Way, Lower 
Village Development Area. Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission 
review the Canyons Village Parking Garage Low Impact Permit , consider Staff’s analysis and the 
CVMA recommendation, conduct a public hearing, and forward a positive recommendatio n to 
the Community Development Director , based on the findings of fact, conclusion of law and 
conditions of approval, outlined in the staff report.   
 
Staff has scheduled the item before the Planning Commission to provide the opportunity for 
public input from the community. 
 
Project Description 
 
Project Name: Canyons Village Parking Garage 
Low Impact Permit  
Project #:    23-191 
Applicant(s): Spencer White , Laurel Simpson  and Sean Thompson, authorized 
representatives 
Property Owners:   TCFC GarageCo, LP 
Property Address:  1840 Ozzy Way, Lower Village Development Area 
Parcel Number:  LVDAM-LV5-AM, 5.84 acres 
(portions of PP-102-C-2-C, PP-102-B-12, PP-102-B-3-A, PP-102-B-3 
and PP-PW-1-610-A) 
Type of Process:  Administrative 
Final Land Use Authority: Community Development Director  
 
Proposal 
 
The applicants, representing TCFC GarageCo, LP , are requesting a Low Impact Permit  approval 
for the Canyons Village Parking Garage , located at 1840 Ozzy Way in the Lower Village 
Development Areat at Canyons Village.  The proposed parking structure  and contiguous  
56
Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 12, 2024 
surface parking will be located on parcel LV5 and will provide 1,840 
parking stalls EXHIBIT A. The entire str ucture is 5 -levels, however, 
due to the existing topography, sloping approximately 26’ feet  
from north to south, the southern end of the structure but only be 
3-levels abo ve grade, while the north end will be 5 -levels above 
grade and will walk out directly to the Cabriolet plaza level. 
 
The Canyons Village Parking Garage  wil l be a multi -year 
construction project.  T he applicant has provided an outline of the 
anticipated phasing EXHIBIT B. 
 
• Phase 1 includes planning approv als, permitting, site work and construction of the first 
three (3) levels of the parking structure.  This phase is intended to create two parking 
levels that would be operational for the 2024/2025 ski seasons.  The third floor would 
act as the parking structure roof.  There will be a temporary surface parking area on the 
adjacent lot, LVDAM-1B-2-AM, to accommodate summer resort activities. 
• Phase 2 includes completion of the remaining two  (2) floors, all architectural finishes 
and final landscaping installation. The garage will be complete and fully operational by 
the 2025/2026 ski season.

Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 12, 2024 
Background 
 
The Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement anticipated a parking  structure 
and/or parking lot  to be located on parcels LV1B and LV5.  The intent is to provide day skier 
parking adjacent to the Cabriolet and capture vehicles at the entrance of the Resort, reducing 
traffic on Canyons Resort Drive and into the Resort Core.    
 
 
On November 16,  2023, an application was received for a Low Impact Permit  for approval, as 
required by the Development Agreement for the proposed parking structure  for LV5 . The 
application was unable to move forward  at that time bec ause the LV5 parcel had not been 
platted, entitling the use.   
 
On November 16, 2023,  an administrative amendment  application was received to modify  
the Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement requesting a modification to 
the maximum height allowed for the LV5 development parcel.  Per the 2018 Amended and 
Restated Canyons Development Agreement,  the  allowed height for LV5  is a maximum of 
6,780’ ASL.  There are some limited exceptions that are allowed to exceed the height 
standard such as antennas, chimney flus,  venting, elevator penthouses, m echanical 
equipment, and rooftop equipment for “green Initiatives ”. During the design process for the 
garage, the upper le vel did not roof  cap.  However , after considering inclusion of a roof the 
applicants state “taking into account a ﬂat roof structure, and the use of tapered insulation to 
provide slope for drainage, it would be feasible to construct a roof over the garage with an 
additional 3' of height though 4' would allow for more aggressive drainage slopes.  It has been 
investigated to lower the garage in lieu of exploring the Amendment for additional height. This 
option would result in essentially pushing the garage down into a hole that would necessitate 
the employment of stairs and ramps to access the cabriolet and associated plaza. The current 
design allows patrons to exit out from the garage at grade, engaging with the plaza. ”  
Therefore, the application was made to request the amendment. 
 
On January 9, 2024, a Plat Amendment and Final Subdivision Plat application was submitted to 
create the LV5 development parcel.   
 
On February 27, 2024, the Snyd erville Basin Planning Commission  recommended approval of 
the Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amen dment and LV 1B and LV5 
Subdivision.  On March 5, 2024, the Summit County Manger conducted a public hearing and 
approved the Lower  Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amen dment and LV 1B and 
LV5 Subdivision.  
 
On March 6, 2024, Community Development Director, Pat Putt, formally approved the 
Administrative Amendment for the Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement 
Land Use and Zoning Table for LV5.   The approval amends the maximum height for LV5 from 
6,780’ ASL to be a maximum height 6,784’ ASL, for the garage only EXHIBIT C.  
58
Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 12, 2024 
 
Analysis and Findings 
 
Section 10-3-4(E) of the Snyderville Basin Development Code requires that any use qualifying 
for a Low Impact Permit must meet the following criteria: 
 
Criteria 1: The use does not significantly increase vehicular traffic, unless the increases are 
consistent with previously approved plans for which appropriate mitigation has been 
contemplated and which has been implemented in an appropriate manner to accommodate 
the proposed amendment.  
 
Analysis: The proposed Canyons Village Parking Garage  will not significantly increase 
vehicular traffic at Canyons Village. The garage is intended to capture existing traffic 
that would otherwise be driving up Canyons Resort Drive and into the Resort Core.  
Ongoing transportation options and mitigation are required as part of the current  
Canyons Transportation plan and Transit Agreement. The parking structure is part of the 
mitigation plan. 
 
Criteria 2: The use does not significantly increase the demand for parking; unless the increases 
are consistent with previously approved plans for which appropriate mitigation has been 
contemplated and which has been implemented in an appropriate manner to accommodate 
the proposed amendment.  
 
Analysis: The proposed Canyons Village Parking  Garage/Parking Area has been 
anticipated since the original 1998 DA was approved.  The garage  will not significantly 
increase the demand for parking in Canyons Village.  There are currently 2,093 stalls 
within the Resort Core and the Lower Village area.  Except for  the existing Pendry hotel 
133 underground parking spaces, 10 surface spaces at the Medical Clinic  and the Vail 
Team build ing (Lower Vil lage) 108 surface parking sp aces, all other surface parking in 
the Resort Core, RC16, RC21 and RC22, will be relocated into the parking garage EXHIBIT 
D. 
 
Criteria 3: The use does not intensify the likelihood of pedestrian and vehicular conflicts.  
 
Analysis: The applicant has considered traffic flow patterns for ingress and egress to try 
and limit the potential conflicts with pedestrians and vehicles.  A conscious decision was 
made not include a sidewalk along Ozzy Way , directly adjacent to the surface parking 
and the two entry points to the garage , to try and minim ize the i nteraction between 
pedestrians and vehicles.  The egress from the parking structure will be limited to a right 
turn only.  The existing crosswalk from the golf maintenance and team building will be 
relocated on the south side of this  egress to try and minimize pedestrian conflicts for 
those crossing from the west side of Lower Village Road.  The egress will cross Millenium 
Trail.  No vertical vegetation has been located along this area in order to prevent 
potential site line impairment for pedestrians or vehicle operators.  
 
59
Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 12, 2024 
Criteria 4: The use does not create unsightly conditions, including, but not limited to, 
unscreened storage and other environmental concerns.  
 
Analysis: Staff does not anticipate unsightly conditions or environmental concerns.  
COMPLIES  
 
Criteria 5: Buildings and other structures shall provide a human scale consistent with adjacent 
development and appropriate to residential uses and consistent with adjacent conforming 
development in the zoning districts. The massing, scale, and architectural design shall be 
consistent with the design guidelines established in Section 10- 4-19 of the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code.

consistent with the architectural design rec ommendations, the ensuring a high level of 
design and consistency with the Canyons Village area. COMPLIES

Analysis: There are no anticipated odor impacts from the proposed project. Staff does 
not anticipate intensification of noise or odors. COMPLIES

adequately mitigated.

Findings of Fact: 
 
1. TCFC GarageCo, LP  is the fee title o wner of parc els PP-102-C-2-C, PP-102-B-12, PP-102-
B-3-A, PP-102-B-3 and PP-PW-1-610-A, creating lot LVDAM-LV5-AM. 
2. The Canyons Amended and R estated Development Agreement allocated LV5 with 
parking/parking garage use that could be a maximum of 6780’ ASL. 
3. On November 16, 2023, a Low Impact Permit was submitted for the proposed Canyons 
Village Parking Garage.   
4. On November 16, 2023, an Administrative  Amendment to the Canyons Amended and 
Restated Development Agreement to allow for an additional four (4 ’) of height for the 
parking structure was submitted to the Community Development Director. 
5. On January 9 , 2024, a Plat Amendment and Final Subdivision Plat application was 
submitted to create the LV5 development parcel. 
6. On February 27, 2024, the Snyd erville Basin Planning Commission  recommended 
approval of the Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amen dment and 
LV1B and LV5 Subdivision .  On March 5, 2024, the Summit County Manger conducted a 
public hearing and approved the Lower  Village Master Development Area Master Plat 
Amendment and LV1B and LV5 Subdivision.  
7. On March 6, 2024, Community Development Director, Pat Putt , formally approved the 
Administrative Amendment for the Canyons Amended and Restated Development 
Agreement Land Use and Zoning Table for LV5.   The approval amends the maximum 
height for LV5 from 6,780’ ASL to be a maximum height 6,784’ ASL, for the garage only. 
8. The Snyderville Basin Development Code Section 10 -3-4(C.2) states; In proposals where 
the CDD or designated planning staff member determines that potential issues may arise 
or additional comment is needed from the community, a public hearing on the 
application may be scheduled with the planning commission. The applicant shall receive 
notice of the hearing. Following the public hearing, the planning commission shall make 
a recommendation to the CDD or designated planning staff member regarding an 
approval, approval with conditions or denial of the application. 
9. Staff has scheduled the item before the Planning Commission as a public hearing to 
provide the opportunity for public input from the community. 
10. Postcard notices announcing the public hearing were mailed to property owners within 
1,000 feet of the subject parcels. 
11. Public notice of the public hearing was published in The Park Record on March 2, 2024. 
12. Staff has reviewed the proposal for compliance with applicable Development Code 
standards. 
13. Service providers have reviewed the proposal for compliance with applicable standards.

Development Agreement and is a vital piece of the Canyons Transportation Plan and 
ongoing transit mitigation efforts. 
15. The project is consistent with the Global Principals outlined in the Canyons 
Development Agreement.

1. The proposed project, as conditioned, meets the requisite standards set forth in the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code, Section 10-3-4: Low Impact Permit. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
 
1. Construction of the project shall follow the approved plans submitted to the 
Community Development Department.  Changes to the plans are subject to an 
amendment of the Low Impact Permit. 
2. No modification to this Low Impact Permit is permitted without the prior approval of 
the Summit County Community Development Department.   
3. All lighting and landscaping details and compliance verification must be reviewed 
and approved by the Summit County Planning prior to issuance of the garage 
building permit.  
4. A Construction Mitigation, including a detailed  phasing and staging plan shall be 
finalized prior to issuance of a developm ent permit.  This should include a detailed 
re-vegetation and re-establishment plan for all disturbed areas that are not included 
in the landscaping plan. A bond in the amount of which includes erosion control , re-
vegetation, re -establishment of temporary parking area and stag ing areas and 
landscaping must be submitted to the County Engineer, prior to any development. 
5. Stormwater Pollution and Prevention Plan and Erosion Control Plan shall be 
followed, and all necessary bonding shall be submitted to Summit County prior to 
any development activity associated with the project.   
6. A current Notice of Intent must be filed and provided to the County Engineer, prior 
to commencement of development.   
7. All conditions of the Canyons DRC must be met. 
8. All requirements of the applicable service providers shall be followed.  
9. All necessary permits must be obtained prior to the commencement of any 
construction activity including, but not limited to , building permits and engineering 
permits.   
10. The Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amendment and LV1B and 
LV5 Subdivision must be recorded prior to any development activity on site. 
11. All existing surface parking  areas within the R esort Core on development parcels 
RC16B, RC21 and RC22 shall  no longer be utilized for parking, upon completion of 
the parking garage. 
12. A certificate of elevation must be submitted to verify compliance with the maximum 
allowed height. 
13. The applicant and garage operator shall co ntinue to work with the Transportation 
Department and High Valley Transit  to discuss mitigation strategies, operations and 
transit routing for the garage and transportation improvements in future phases.

66
The following describes the parking garage to be designed and built in Canyons Village adjacent to the bottom 
terminal of the cabriolet lift.  Overall, it is expected the project will deliver an efficient, effective, and safe parking 
facility that results in a suitable visitor experience as allowed by the site, budget, and schedule.   
The project will consist of ﬁve levels of parking with a roof (if the SPA administrative amendment is approved).  
There is an integrated guest drop off zone on the north end of level 2 as well as vehicular access points on levels 2 
and 3.  Pedestrian access to the cabriolet and adjacent Transit Center is via the plaza on Level 1.  Vertical circulation 
is provided by 6 full-height stair towers, 2 elevators, and a bank of escalators on the east side that run from Level 5 
the Level 1.  The stairs and elevators are fully enclosed while the escalators are protected from the elements but 
open to the interior.  
The structure is II-B construction using long-span concrete beams and is naturally ventilated per IBC requirements.   
The facade has extensive aesthetic treatments beﬁtting its location adjacent to the entry of the resort. 
The project is proposing a low slope roof structure with vertical circulation elements extending above said low 
slope roof for articulation of the design.  Though in slight excess of the prescribed height, the beneﬁts to the 
community are: 
• Compliance with Dark Skies while ensuring proper lighting for guest safety. 
• The elimination of the need to snow melt level 5. 
• Shielding the parking from those uphill from the site. 
• Improved safety due to reduced potential for slip and falls.

PRODUCT FEATURES:
 » Unique hexagonal light guide with edge-lit LED light engine provides three distribution options - 
Type V , Rectangular and Drive Lane
 » Lightweight construction (7 lbs) for ease of installation
 » Nominal output range of 4,000 to 10,000 lm
 » Compatible with TekLink™  lighting control technology

SPECIFICATIONS
HOUSING: Die-cast aluminum and UV-stabilized polycarbonate construction. Standard TGIC polyester powder coat /f_inish on aluminum components with /f_ive-step pre-treatment to withstand 1,000 
hour salt spray test per ASTM B117. See Ordering Information for available /f_inishes. Closed-cell gasketing seals all housing component interfaces. 
MOUNTING: Direct-to-Surface (DTS) mounts over recessed electrical box to ceiling, Quick-Mount (QM) mounts to either surface or recessed electrical box, Trunnion Mount (TK) or pendant-mounted 
(PM) via ¾" rigid sealed conduit. See Ordering Information for selection. Fixture weight is 7 lbs.
OPTICAL: Type V-Wide (5W), Rectangular Wide (RW) and Drive Lane (DL) optical distribution options.  Light guide outer lens constructed of clear UV-stabilized polycarbonate.
ELECTRICAL: Edge-lit mid-power LED light source. See Ordering Information for color temperature options. 80 CRI minimum. 120-277 VAC, 347VAC and 480VAC 50/60Hz input; constant-current 
dimming driver; <20% THD, >0.90 PF . Minimum 83% electrical ef/f_iciency. 0-10V dimming protocol with 1-100% range and dim-to-dark capabilities, 0.2mA source current. Surge and transient 
protection rated at 6kV per ANSI C62.41-2002 and 10kV per ANSI C82.77-5 2015.  20kA max. transient protection (L-N). Replaceable surge suppressor. Driver is EMC compliant with FCC 47 CFR 
Part 15, Class B. Passes IEC 61000-4-4 EFT and IEC 61000-4-5 (Class 4) surge evaluation.
TEKLINK
™ TL50M/TL50DM: An independent lighting control system integral to the luminaire, featuring its own embedded occupancy sensor and adjustable time-out/dimming settings. TL50DM 
adds a closed-loop daylight harvesting function for maximum energy savings. 
TEKLINK
™ TL100: Centralized, wired zonal occupancy and closed-loop daylight harvesting control system. TL100 controllers are standalone devices and ordered separately from luminaire.  
Click here for speci/f_ications.
TEKLINK TL200M/TL200DM: A wireless, zonal occupancy control system (BLE v5.2) with embedded sensor. Mobile app for Android and iOS device allows changes to time-out period, turn-off delay 
and light level. System commissioning is not required - network is operational upon power-up. TL200DM adds the daylight harvesting function for maximum energy savings.
TEKLINK
™ TL1000/2000: An adaptive lighting control system with wired or wireless communication between system nodes. In addition to occupancy detection and daylight harvesting, TL1000 and 
TL2000 feature advanced scheduling and energy management capabilities with cloud-based management of system settings, reporting and noti/f_ications.
SENSOR & CONTROLS: Optional sensor available with compatible third party controls. To see the full list of compatible controls, click here.
PHOTOMETRICS: Photometry tested to the IESNA LM-79-08 standard by an ILAC/ISO17025 accredited laboratory. For photometric data, please go to www.kenall.com. 
WARRANTY: Limited /f_ive (5) year warranty. Peace of Mind Guarantee
®  when ordered and installed with direct-to-surface (DTS) mounting.
LISTINGS: Luminaire is certi/f_ied to UL Standards by Intertek Testing Laboratory for Wet Location. IP66 rating per IEC 60598 standard. Suitable for installation into -35°C to 40°C ambient 
environments, unless otherwise noted. Passes 3G vibration test per ANSI C136.31-2001.

Memorandum:
The parking garage project is planned to take place in two phases.  Per discussions with Summit 
County these two phases will now be formally submitted in two unique bid sets for permitting.  
At a high level the phases break down as follows:
Phase I - March to November, 2024.  Work to be included; mass excavation, shoring wall 
construction, site grading, site utilities, construction of levels 1-3 of the parking garage.
Phase II - March to November 2025.  Work to be included; construction of levels 4-5 and 
the roof of the garage, all facade materials, elevated drop off lane and mass timber roof 
structure, completion of all vertical circulation including completion of stairs and 
erection of escalators and elevators, associated enclosures, all surface paving and 
pedestrian circulation.  
Further delineation of the two scopes of work can be found on the attached exiting plans, ﬂoor 
plans, site plans and elevations for Phase I and II, attached.
Please ﬁnd following responses to the speciﬁc follow up questions included in the February 29, 
2024 email;
1. When do you anticipated commencement of site grading?  Response: Wadsworth Brothers is
anticipating pulling a construction permit by March 28, 2024 at which time excavation will
commence.
2. You mentioned that you will be moving 40,000 yards.  Where will this be going?  Based on the cut/ﬁll
analysis, not much will remain onsite.  Will it be used in the resort?  Response: A Browns Canyon pit
X Email FTP Hand Mail Other
Recipient Company Email/Address
Tiffanie Robinson Summit County trobinson@summitcounty.org
Laurel Simpson TCFC lsimpson@tc-fc.com
Spencer White white.spencer@gmail.com
Mark Oakeson Wadsworth Brothers moakeson@wadsbro.com
Project Name: TCFC Parking Garage Date: 2024-03-04

EXHIBIT B1
110
located off of Browns Canyon Road is the intended destination for the spoils generated from the 
mass excavation.
3. When do you anticipate going vertical?  Just clarifying, you indicated in your response that you will 
obtain two (2) separate building permits, correct?  Response: It is anticipated the project would “go 
vertical” about April 29.   Wadsworth Brothers would initially pull the previously discussed 
construction permit to allow for excavation to commence while the building permit review process 
is being completed.  Then a Phase I Building Permit would be obtained for the ’24 construction 
season and a subsequent permit would be pulled for the Phase II construction (the balance of the 
work) to be conducted and completed in the ’25 construction season.  
4. When will all architectural elements be completed?   Response: All architectural elements would be 
completed by the 25/26 ski season.
5. Landscaping (will this be phased?) [sic}   Response: Landscaping would be completed as part of the 
Phase II package. 
6. Sidewalk/circulation improvements (explain temporary and permanent)  - FYI, this will be a big 
discussion point for the SBPC given this was a primary concern in 2017 for the Employee Housing 
access.  Response: The project would maintain pedestrian circulation on and around the site 
differently depending upon the time of year.  During the construction season public access would 
be stopped across the site.  Outside of these two construction seasons public access around and 
through the site would be maintained.  Please refer to the attached pedestrian circulation and 
construction phasing plans.
7. Temporary laydown area – this is new to me. When will it be utilized (summer of 2024 or not until ski 
seasons)?  When will it be removed and reestablished? Lighting? Bonding?  Response: The laydown 
areas shown will be used during Phase I construction and then reestablished after Phase I is 
completed for use during the 2024-2025 ski season.  All space designated as laydown will be 
occupied from about April 15, 2024 through November 30, 2024 and then will be reestablished with 
the same parking surface materials as are currently there.  
8. Anything else you think is important to include that I may have missed. Response: No other items 
come to mind at this time.  If something does come up while writing your report please do not 
hesitate to call me at 801-971-8260 (cell).

Landscape Narrative:
The landscape plan for the TCFC Parking Structure will comply with the SPA Design
Guidelines for the Lower Village at the Canyons Village at the Park City Resort.  The
plants specified In the plan will be native to the high mountain climate of the resort.
They will be sensitive to creating a natural looking landscape that can be enjoyed by
both people living in the development and passing by.  A landscape buffer will be
created between the structure and the existing residential areas.  Landscape material
will also be proposed along Canyons Resort Drive.

sent via email: lsimpson@tc-fc.com 
RE: Administrative Amendment - Amended and Restated Development Agreement for the 
Canyons Specially Planned Area, Land Use and Zoning Table for parcel LV-5, file #23-192. 
Dear Ms. Simpson, 
On November 16, 2019, an application was submitted for an Administrative Amendment 
modifying the Amended and Restated Development Agreement for the Canyons Specially 
Planned Area (the "Development Agreement"), Land Use and Zoning Table for Parcel LV-5 
pursuant to Section 5.13.2. The application requests the allowance of four (4') feet of 
additional height for the LV5 parcel. The current Land Use and Zoning Table specifies that 
Parcel LV-5 is allowed a parking/parking garage with a maximum height of 6,780' ASL. Your 
request will amend the Land Use and Zoning Table to authorize a maximum height of 6,784' 
ASL for the parking structure only. Your application states, "As currently designed, the top of 
the Level 5 structural slab is at an altitude of 6770'-0" ASL. The roofs over the stairs are designed 
to 6780'-0" ASL and we have shown elevator overruns per the SPA. Vail requires 9'-0" clear to 
accommodate roof boxes, etc. and the depth of the structural system is 3'. Taking into account a 
flat roof structure, and the use of tapered insulation to provide slope for drainage, it would be 
feasible to construct a roof over the garage with an additional 3' of height though 4' would allow 
for more aggressive drainage slopes. It has been investigated to lower the garage in lieu of 
exploring the Amendment for additional height. This option would result in essentially pushing 
the garage down into a hole that would necessitate the employment of stairs and ramps to access 
the cabriolet and associated plaza. The current design allows patrons to exit out from the garage 
at grade, engaging with the plaza." 
Additional considerations for the additional four (4') feet of height amendment include: 
• Compliance with Dark Skies while ensuring proper lighting for guest safety.
• Furthering the sustainability goals of the Canyons and Summit County by the elimination of
the need for a natural gas snowmelt system on level 5.
• Protection of viewshed by shielding the parking from those uphill from the site.
• Improved safety due to reduced potential for slip and falls.
Please accept this letter as official confirmation that I have approved the Administrative
Development Agreement Amendment for the parcel LV5 change in height from 6,780' ASL to
COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT DEPARTMENT- PLANNING DIVISION 
P.O. Box 128 
60 NORTH MAIN STREET 
COALVILLE, UT 84017

Dear Service Providers,
 
The Community Development Department is in receipt a Low Impact Permit application for Canyons Village Parking Garage, located at 3483 Lower Village Road at Canyons
Village. Per the Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement, parcel LV5 is allowed:
 
Project Details:
Project Name:             Canyons Village Parking Garage Low Impact Permit
Project #
         
 
           
 
 
 23-191
Property Owner(s):
    
 TCFC GarageCo LP
Parcel Address:
          
 Approx. 3900 Lower Village Road, Canyons Village
Parcel #:
                     
 
 
 LV5 – (as shown on the Lower Village Development Area Master Plat Amended LV1B and LV5 Subdivision being reviewed simultaneously)
Zoning:
                         
 SPA
Proposed Application: Low Impact Permit
 
The structure is proposed to be 5 levels, with a maximum height of 6784’ ASL.  The applicants have submitted an Administrative Amendment to request the additional 4’ of
height beyond the current allowed maximum of 6,780’ ASL.
 
 The additional height is due to the cap on the upper level which is intended to help comply with dark sky
ordinances while providing necessary lighting for safety, eliminate the need for snow melt on level 5, shield parking for those uphill and improve overall safety due to
reduced potential for slip and falls. The parking will be distributed as follows:
Level 1: 333 Parking Spaces
Level 2: 317 Parking Spaces
Level 3: 355 Parking Spaces
Level 4: 364 Parking Spaces
Level 5: 358 Parking Spaces
Surface: 113 Parking Spaces
Total: 1,840 Parking Spaces
 
The applicant is proposing to construct the garage in two (2) phases, beginning in the spring of 2024 and completing it winter of 2025.   They intend to construct the first
three (3) stories of the structure in 2024 and request a temporary c/o to utilize the first 2 levels of the parking structure for the 2024/2025 ski season the 3rd level would act
as a roof, completing the remainder of the structure by the 2025/2026 ski season. The proposed phasing/temporary occupancy details are included.
 
Your input in valuable and important to the review process. Please provide comments by Wednesday, January 24, 2024. If we do not hear from you by this date, we will
assume you have no concerns with this application. In the meantime, if you have any questions, please feel free to contact me directly.
 
Kind Regards,
Tiffanie N. Robinson, Senior Planner
Summit County Community Development

Date: Tuesday, January 23, 2024 3:25:41 PM
Attachments: image003.png
Hi Tiffanie,
 
The Engineering Department needs the following comments addressed:
1. Please provide a geotechnical report
2. Please provide drainage calculations (Ryan Cathy Letter)
3. Road / Parking Lot Design:
a. Please provide driveway dimensions including widths and approaches (Lower Village Road and Ozzy Way)
b. Please provide driveway slopes on to Ozzy way (must be <3% slope for 100’)
c. Access on Lower Village Road needs to be located minimum spacing of 35’ from opposite intersection (see Summit County Code Section 10-4-10).
d. Please show right of way (including width) of Lower Village Road, Ozzy Way and Canyons Resort Drive.
4. ADA Details:
a. Only 13 ADA stalls are shown on Level 1, plans say there are 14 (as required by Federal ADA code)
b. Please show dimensions of ADA stalls and access isle, both normal and van accessible stalls
c. Please show slopes of ADA stalls and access routes
d. Please show ADA access routes
5. Utility Details:
a. Please call out pipe size and detail on storm drainage system
6. Erosion Control/SWPPP Details:
a. Please show erosion control (silt fence, straw wattle, etc.) on plans.
b. Stabilized construction entrance with specification notes
c. Location of dumpster, toilet, staging area, concrete washout, and construction parking
Please let me know if you have any questions or concerns.  Thank you.
 
Regards,

Zoning:
                         
 SPA
Proposed Application: Low Impact Permit
 
The structure is proposed to be 5 levels, with a maximum height of 6784’ ASL.
 
 The applicants have submitted an Administrative Amendment to request the additional 4’ of
height beyond the current allowed maximum of 6,780’ ASL.
 
 The additional height is due to the cap on the upper level which is intended to help comply with dark sky
ordinances while providing necessary lighting for safety, eliminate the need for snow melt on level 5, shield parking for those uphill and improve overall safety due to
reduced potential for slip and falls. The parking will be distributed as follows:
Level 1: 333 Parking Spaces
Level 2: 317 Parking Spaces
Level 3: 355 Parking Spaces
Level 4: 364 Parking Spaces
Level 5: 358 Parking Spaces
Surface: 113 Parking Spaces
Total: 1,840 Parking Spaces
 
The applicant is proposing to construct the garage in two (2) phases, beginning in the spring of 2024 and completing it winter of 2025.
 
 
 
They intend to construct the first
three (3) stories of the structure in 2024 and request a temporary c/o to utilize the first 2 levels of the parking structure for the 2024/2025 ski season the 3rd level would act
as a roof, completing the remainder of the structure by the 2025/2026 ski season. The proposed phasing/temporary occupancy details are included.
 
Your input in valuable and important to the review process.
 
Please provide comments by Wednesday, January 24, 2024. If we do not hear from you by this date, we will
assume you have no concerns with this application. In the meantime, if you have any questions, please feel free to contact me directly.
 
Kind Regards,
Tiffanie N. Robinson, Senior Planner
Summit County Community Development

Landscape Narrative:
The landscape plan for the TCFC Parking Structure will comply with the SPA Design
Guidelines for the Lower Village at the Canyons Village at the Park City Resort. The
plants specified In the plan will be native to the high mountain climate of the resort.
They will be sensitive to creating a natural looking landscape that can be enjoyed by
both people living in the development and passing by. A landscape buffer will be
created between the structure and the existing residential areas. Landscape material
will also be proposed along Canyons Resort Drive.

Canyons Parking Garage – Storm Drain Conformance Review 
Laurel,
We are submitting this letter to confirm that the storm drain design at the Canyons Parking Garage is in 
conformance with the current CVMA Storm Drain Master Plan. The proposed work consists of demolishing 
the existing Lower Canyons Parking Lot, construction of a new parking garage and supplementary service 
lot, and associated road/utility connections. Storm water is currently managed by a single inlet located on 
the northeast corner of the parking lot, which drains to a 24” storm drainpipe that ultimate discharges into 
the Hole 15 detention pond. Stormflows that do not reach this inlet are either discharged onto Canyons 
Resort Drive, the bus drop off loop behind the 7-11 Convenience store, or the Hole 15 golf course.
This conformance review is based on the TCC proposed design as of 02/01/2024. Because the new parking 
garage location sits upon an existing parking lot, it was found that the impervious area from the proposed 
layout is very close to the impervious area that exists now. We have calculated that the new site plan will 
add approximately 11,000 square feet additional impervious area, ultimately draining to the Hole 15 Pond. 
After consideration of the information mentioned above, the master plan model was updated accordingly. 
Results from the model indicate that the 11,000 square feet of additional impervious area will require an 
additional 1,010 cubic feet of detention volume in the Hole 15 Pond during the 100-year, 24-hour storm.
Furthermore, The Utah Division of Water Quality has implemented a requirement to retain and infiltrate 
the 80th percentile storm event. In Summit County this results in 0.47” per square foot. The total disturbed 
area of the project site is 338,882 square feet, or approximately 7.78 acres. It is calculated that 13,273 cubic 
feet of volume must be allocated for the retention burden.
According to the master plan records, the Hole 15 pond has 23,100 cubic feet of unallocated detention 
volume, and 44,887 cubic feet of unallocated retention volume. With approval from the CVMA, the Hole 
15 pond can accommodate the detention and retention burden of the Canyons Parking Garage bringing its 
total unallocated detention volume to 22,090 cubic feet, and its total unallocated retention volume to 31,614 
cubic feet.
Sunrise Gondola Stormwater Calculations Summary:
Detention Volume Required: 1,010 cubic feet.
Retention Volume Required: 13,273 cubic feet.
Total Volume Required: 14,283 cubic feet.
In summary, results from the model show the proposed Canyons Parking Garage stormwater design meets 
the parameters set forth by Ordinance 381-A once volume requirements are met.

1.0 INTRODUCTION
The purpose of this report is to describe the basis stormwater system design for the proposed parking 
garage at The Canyons and provide supporting calculations that demonstrate that the design is 
satisfactory. The project is located at the Canyons Ski Resort, at the intersection of Canyons Resort Drive 
and Lower Village Road in Park City, Utah. 
2.0 METHODOLOGY
The criteria for the storm drain system design are based on Summit County Ordinance 381-A (and 
appendices) whose key design considerations include but are not limited to: 
• Appendix A, page A-15 – Peak Flow Rate Criteria
o Flow rate is not to exceed 10-year and 100-year pre-developed flows.
o Detention ponds are to have a spillway designed to pass the entire 100-year flow.
o The 10-year storm shall be used to size underground pipe conveyance systems.
o All other storm water structures shall be used to safely pass the 100-year storm.
o Only methods identified in Appendix F shall be used for calculations.
• Appendix F – Calculating Peak Discharge and Volume 
o Acceptable calculation methods include the Rational Method and the TR-55 Method.
o Soil data shall be gathered from the website http:/soildatamart.nrcs.usda.gov/ (page 
F-2)
o The runoff Rational C Coefficient tables F1 and F2 on page F-3 shall be used.
o Time of Concentration (T) Equation on page F-4 shall be used
▪ T=1.8*(1.1-c)D0.5/S1/3
▪ Minimum T=10 minutes
The software used to perform the drainage calculations is Bentley Haestad’s SewerGEMS, utilizing physical 
attributes derived from a topographic LiDAR survey, and proposed storm drain design. NOAA Atlas 14 
precipitation intensity tables with a latitude and longitude corresponding to the Canyons Ski Resort was 
used as the basis of storm data within the hydraulic model. See Table 1 below & the Appendix.

EXHIBIT H33
177
TALISMAN CIVIL CONSULTANTS, LLC
1588 South Main St, Suite 200, Salt Lake City, UT, 84115 | 801.743.1300 | www.talismancivil.com
The storm water model utilizes the Soil Conservation Service Method (commonly known as SCS or TR-55 
Method) to analyze the hydrology of the site. The SCS method is briefly summarized below:  
• The basis of the curve number method is the empirical relationship between the retention 
(rainfall not converted into runoff), hydrologic properties of the watershed, and rainfall 
intensity/distribution. It accounts for most runoff producing watershed characteristics e.g.: 
soil type, land use/treatment, surface condition, and antecedent moisture condition.
• Composite Curve Numbers (CN Values) were developed by reviewing impervious, and 
pervious areas.
• The NOAA Atlas 14 precipitation data was used to create SCS Type II & storm distribution 
hydrograph curves.
3.0 ANALYSIS & RESULTS
Existing Conditions
The “Lower Canyons Parking Lot” currently exists at the project site. The lot is approximately 223,000 SF 
(5.11 acres), made of asphalt and is impervious. The soil classification for the site is found to be Hydrologic 
Soil Group C.  The general topography of the site slopes southwest to northeast at a consistent 6% to 7%.
Storm water is currently managed by a single inlet located on the northeast corner of the parking lot, 
which drains to a 24” storm drainpipe that ultimate discharges into the Hole 15 detention pond. 
Stormflows that do not reach this inlet are either discharged onto Canyons Resort Drive, the bus drop off 
loop behind the 7-11 Convenience store, or the Hole 15 golf course.
Proposed Conditions: Catchment Areas
The site is broken down into 12 catchment areas. Each catchment area was delineated according to the 
existing topography, and proposed grading and drainage plans. The total tributary area directing flow to 
storm drain infrastructure equates to approximately 264,500 square feet. Per Ordinance 381-A, the 
minimum time of concentration of 10 minutes was used for each catchment. 
Each catchment area is characterized by an SCS curve number, with higher numbers indicating a higher 
percentage of impervious surfaces and/or more impervious soils, resulting in increased storm runoff. 
Conversely, lower curve numbers signify reduced runoff, as water either infiltrates directly into the ground 
or encounters vegetation, such as grasses and shrubbery.
The parking structure roof, open air parking lot, road, sidewalks, and other hardscaped areas are 100% 
impervious, with a curve number set at 98. In contrast, the curve number for the surrounding landscape 
is defined at 70, consistent for Type C soils with natural brush weed grass mixture cover types of fair 
condition. 
See the Catchment Tables in the Appendix for a tabular summary of each catchment area including total 
area, SCS curve number and time of concentration. 
4
EXHIBIT H34
178
TALISMAN CIVIL CONSULTANTS, LLC
1588 South Main St, Suite 200, Salt Lake City, UT, 84115 | 801.743.1300 | www.talismancivil.com
Proposed Conditions: Conveyance
The storm drain system is separated into two different sub-networks:
• The Area well, Parking Lot & Roadway sub-network
• Roof Drain sub-network
The two sub-networks are separated by a sand oil/separator located in the emergency vehicle access on 
the northeast side of the garage. Storm water from the Area Wells, Southern Parking Lot, and Roadways 
are routed upstream of the sand/oil separator. The conveyance system from the roof drains are then tied 
into the system downstream of the sand/oil separator.
The proposed pipe sizes vary from 10”, 12”, 18, & 24” in diameter, with a minimum slope of 0.5%. The 
area well drains are  modeled at 10” with a minimum slope of 0.25%. The area well drains are to be 
perforated pipe, typical of wall drainage design. The proposed storm drain system then ties into an 
existing catch basin with a 24” outlet found east of the garage, and ultimately discharges to the Hole 15 
Pond. Results from the model indicate the storm drain design will adequately convey the 10 year, 24-hour 
storm.
See the Conduit Tables in the Appendix for a tabular summary of each pipe area including diameter, slope, 
maximum flow, and capacity. 
 
Proposed Conditions: Retention
The Utah Division of Water Quality has implemented a requirement to retain and infiltrate the 80th 
percentile storm event. In Summit County this results in 0.47” per square foot. The total disturbed area 
of the project site is 338,882 square feet, or approximately 7.78 acres. It is calculated that 13,273 cubic 
feet of volume must be allocated for the retention burden.
According to the master plan records, the Hole 15 Pond Currently has 44,887 cubic feet of unallocated 
retention volume. With approval from the CVMA, the Hole 15 Pond can accommodate the retention 
burden of the Canyons Parking Garage bringing its total unallocated retention volume to 31,614 cubic 
feet. 
Proposed Conditions: Detention
The total existing impervious area from the Lower Canyons Parking Lot is approximately 223,000 square 
feet. The total impervious area from the proposed parking garage and parking lot is approximately 
234,000 square feet, resulting in an additional 11,000 square feet of impervious area.  Results from the 
model show that an additional 1,010 cubic feet of detention volume must be allocated for the detention 
burden.
According to the master plan records, the Hole 15 Pond Currently has 23,100 cubic feet of unallocated 
detention volume. With approval from the CVMA, the Hole 15 Pond can accommodate the detention 
burden of the Canyons Parking Garage bringing its total unallocated detention volume to 22,090 cubic 
feet. 
4.0 Summary
In summary, results from the model show the proposed Canyons Parking Garage stormwater design 
meets the parameters set forth by Ordinance 381-A once volume requirements are met.
